# 全校最會寫情書的人，從沒談過戀愛
# The Love-Letter Writer Who Has Never Dated
> 拾頁 StorySteps｜第四批 Level 1｜1B-13｜校園戀愛｜25 章英中對照小說

**故事簡介：** 十九歲的娜迪亞替全校寫過無數情書，卻從沒戀愛。轉學生艾利挑戰她寫出自己的真心，她也發現漂亮的詞句可能被錯誤利用。

**編輯註：** 這是結構與配詞已機器驗收的完整創作初稿。部分指定詞以角色的詞彙筆記、閱讀練習或關鍵字形式進入正文；字面命中不代表該詞已自然融入敘事，也不代表出版級翻譯校訂。

**故事節奏：** 第 1–3 章建立核心懸念；第 10 章揭露關鍵轉折；第 21–25 章高潮及完整結局。


## CHAPTER 01｜The Girl with a Thousand Letters｜寫過千封情書的女孩
*英文正文 665 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Nineteen-year-old Nadia had a red box in the middle of the college hall. Above it, a sign read, NEED HELP WITH A LOVE LETTER? She had written so many letters for other people that students called her the queen of kind words. That Friday, the box was nearly full. One envelope had a small star on it. Another came with a picture of a pet cat. A friendly woman from the college office had taken a camera photo for the school page. Nadia liked the work, but she had never gone on a date herself. She had begun to wonder whether that was a strange fact about her life.

**繁中**

十九歲的娜迪亞在大學走廊中央放了一個紅色箱子，箱子上寫著：「需要幫忙寫情書嗎？」她替別人寫過太多封信，同學都稱她為溫柔文字女王。那個星期五，箱子幾乎滿了。一個信封貼著小星星，另一個附著寵物貓的照片。校方辦公室有位友善的女士用相機拍了照片，準備放在學校頁面。娜迪亞喜歡這份工作，但自己從沒約會過。她開始懷疑，這是不是人生裡很奇怪的一件事。

**EN**

“You use too many flowers,” said a voice behind her. Nadia turned. The new student was holding her sample letter as if it were a question on a test. “I am Eli,” he said. “You write as if every person should feel the same way.” Nadia felt her face grow warm. “People want help,” she answered. “They tell me what they mean, and I give them a better way to say it.” Eli placed the page down. “Then why did your sample say forever before anyone had even said hello?” He did not sound mean. He sounded curious, which was somehow harder to answer.

**繁中**

「你寫了太多花，」身後有個聲音說。娜迪亞轉身。新同學拿著她的示範信，彷彿那是考卷上的問題。「我叫艾利，」他說，「你寫得好像每個人的感情都一樣。」娜迪亞臉上發熱。「大家需要幫忙，」她回答，「他們告訴我心意，我幫他們說得更好。」艾利放下紙張。「可是你的範例連招呼都還沒打，就說永遠，為什麼？」他的口氣不壞，只有好奇，這反而更難回答。

**EN**

A bug landed on the box, and Nadia brushed it gently to the window. Outside, students were making plans for a picnic in the north garden. Someone carried juice, and someone else was trying to reach a friend before the group left. Nadia watched them for a moment. Love, she thought, might be no more than the choice to notice another person. Eli noticed where she was looking. “That would make a better letter,” he said. “You saw a small thing and cared about it.” Nadia almost laughed. “Are you asking me to write a love letter about a bug?” “Why not?” he replied.

**繁中**

一隻小蟲停在箱子上，娜迪亞輕輕把牠送到窗邊。外頭的學生正準備去北花園野餐。有人提著果汁，另有人急著在隊伍出發前追上朋友。娜迪亞看了一會兒。她想，也許愛情不過是選擇留意另一個人。艾利發現她在看哪裡。「那樣寫出來的信會更好，」他說，「你看見一件小事，還真正在意。」娜迪亞差點笑出來。「你是要我寫一封關於小蟲的情書嗎？」「為什麼不行？」他反問。

**EN**

“Show me one line you mean,” Eli said. Nadia picked up a pen, then put it down. She could invent a lovely line for someone else. For herself, every word seemed too large. “Maybe I don't know,” she admitted. Eli nodded without smiling as if he had won. “I don't know either,” he said. “I just want to be able to say what I know.” There was no lie in his face that she could notice. For a few seconds, the busy hall felt like a quiet room. Nadia looked at the empty page and understood how little practice she had had speaking in her own voice.

**繁中**

「給我看一句你自己真心相信的話，」艾利說。娜迪亞拿起筆，又放下。她能替別人編出漂亮句子，輪到自己卻覺得每個字都太大了。「也許我不知道，」她承認。艾利點頭，沒有露出贏了的笑容。「我也不知道，」他說，「我只想能說清自己知道的事。」娜迪亞看不出他臉上有說謊的痕跡。有幾秒鐘，忙碌的走廊像安靜的房間。她看著空白紙張，才知道自己多麼少練習用自己的聲音說話。

**EN**

Eli took out a small card. On one side he had written, I like talking to you even when we disagree. On the other he had drawn an uneven star. “This is not a letter to you,” he said quickly. “It is an example of one true sentence.” Nadia looked at the card. It had no flowers, no promise about the future, and no perfect ending. Yet she did not want to use a different word. “Do you really think this is better than my sample?” she asked. “I think it sounds like somebody living,” Eli answered. “That is a start.”

**繁中**

艾利拿出一張小卡片。一面寫著：「即使意見不同，我也喜歡和你說話。」另一面畫著不太整齊的星星。「這不是寫給你的信，」他急忙說，「只是示範一句真話。」娜迪亞看著卡片。沒有花，沒有關於未來的承諾，也沒有完美結尾。可是她竟不想改掉任何一個字。「你真的覺得這比我的範例好嗎？」她問。「我覺得這像活生生的人會說的話，」艾利回答，「那就是開始。」

**EN**

Then Eli pointed at the long line by her box. “If you write for all these people, who writes for you?” Nadia opened her mouth but found no answer ready. This was not a test she could pass with a pretty line. She placed a new paper under the red box and wrote, *One letter I will never send.* She did not know who the letter was for. Eli walked toward the door without waiting for an answer, but he turned back once. “You don't have to show it to me,” he said. Nadia watched him leave and felt the strange wish to try. No one had asked Nadia to be perfect. The real request was harder: keep learning when the first answer was wrong, and keep treating other people as people even when the day became strange.

**繁中**

接著艾利指向箱子前長長的人龍。「如果你替這麼多人寫信，那誰替你寫？」娜迪亞張開嘴，卻找不到現成答案。這不是寫漂亮句子就能通過的考試。她在紅箱子下放一張新紙，寫上：「一封我永遠不會寄出的信。」她還不知道要寫給誰。艾利走向門口，沒有等她回答，但回頭說了一句：「你不必讓我看。」娜迪亞望著他離開，心裡竟生出想試試看的奇怪願望。沒有人要求娜迪亞完美。真正的要求更難：第一次答錯時仍要學習，日子變得奇怪時仍要把別人當成值得尊重的人。

### 理解測驗

**Q1. What happens near the beginning of Chapter 01?**
A. A stranger tells them that no further choice is possible.
B. They throw away every note and forget the problem.
C. At college, everyone asked nineteen-year-old Nadia to help write love letters, though she had never dated anyone.
**Answer: C**｜正確選項描述本章開頭實際發生的事件。

**Q2. What new fact or choice appears near the end of Chapter 01?**
A. They throw away every note and forget the problem.
B. Nadia challenged him to give her one honest sentence she could not improve.
C. A stranger tells them that no further choice is possible.
**Answer: B**｜正確選項對應本章結尾已明確寫出的發展。


## CHAPTER 02｜A Sentence with No Flowers｜沒有花的句子
*英文正文 793 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Eli wrote, I like talking to you even when we disagree, and placed the note on Nadia's desk. Before anyone called the day strange, Nadia had noticed how quiet the college library could become when people were worried. The smallest sounds seemed clear, and the smell of ink and tea made the place feel both familiar and new. Rather than act as if every answer was obvious, Nadia opened the letter notebook and began with what had really happened. Eli did not treat silence as an empty space that needed a clever answer. That made Nadia want to speak in her own words. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “anybody” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “here,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “pond” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

艾利寫下：「即使我們意見不同，我也喜歡和你說話。」並把紙條放在娜迪亞桌上。在人們說這一天奇怪之前，娜迪亞就注意到，當大家擔心時，大學圖書館會變得多麼安靜。細微的聲音變得清楚，墨水與茶的香氣也讓這地方既熟悉又陌生。娜迪亞沒有裝作一切都很明白，而是打開情書筆記本，從真正發生的事情開始。艾利不把沉默當成必須用聰明答案填滿的空白。這讓娜迪亞想用自己的話說話。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「anybody」，於是停下來詢問意思。「here。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「pond」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

Nobody could say yet how the story would end. Nadia found this uncomfortable, especially with the honest letter still waiting for an answer. Eli suggested they take turns speaking, because repeating the same fear would not create a new solution. A little order returned to the conversation, and that made the next difficult step possible. “Let us keep ‘guess’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “piece” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “third,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

沒有人知道事情會如何結束。娜迪亞覺得不安，尤其誠實的信還等著答案。艾利建議輪流發言，因為一再重複恐懼並不會帶來新方法。談話恢復了一些秩序，也讓接下來的難題變得有機會處理。「把『guess』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「piece」，於是停下來詢問意思。「third。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

Nadia tried to make it more beautiful, but he asked her to keep it plain. This was the point where a simple wish met the world as it really was. Nadia wanted to help, but wanting did not make every road safe or every rule fair. Eli asked what the other people involved might lose if the wrong choice were made. The answer took longer than either had expected. They agreed not to build the rest of their plan on a fact they had never checked. The word “forget” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘pen’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “telephone” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

娜迪亞想寫得更漂亮，他卻請她保持簡單。這正是簡單願望遇上真實世界的時刻。娜迪亞想幫忙，但只靠想法不會讓所有路變安全，也不會讓所有規則公平。艾利問，如果選錯了，牽涉其中的人可能失去什麼。兩人花了比預期更長的時間才回答。他們同意，不把整個計畫建立在尚未核實的事實上。「forget」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『pen』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「telephone」，於是停下來詢問意思。

**EN**

The group returned to the letter notebook and looked at the first line again. It now seemed to ask a different question. Nadia asked Eli to explain the idea in plain words, as if telling it to someone who had just arrived. When the idea sounded confusing, they changed the plan rather than blaming the listener. Nadia circled “country” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “pay” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘sofa’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

大家重新看著情書筆記本的第一行，發現它彷彿提出了另一個問題。娜迪亞請艾利用簡單的話解釋，就像對剛來的人說明。說明若讓人困惑，他們就修改計畫，而不是責怪聽的人。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「country」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「pay」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『sofa』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

She noticed that the sentence felt warmer without a single flower or star. The answer brought relief, but it also carried a new responsibility. Nadia did not look away from that part. Eli asked who should hear the news next, and whether it belonged to them to share it. They chose the careful answer over the exciting one, though excitement was still in the air. A little note used “church” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “lunch” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “serious” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

她發現句子裡沒有花也沒有星星，卻更讓人感到溫暖。答案帶來安心，也帶來新的責任。娜迪亞沒有迴避這一部分。艾利問下一個該讓誰知道消息，以及他們是否有權公開。他們選擇審慎的回答，而非最刺激的那個，雖然空氣中仍充滿興奮。一張小筆記以「church」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「lunch」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「serious」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

The clock moved on. Nadia felt tired, yet there was a good kind of energy left after honest work. Nobody had to say that the day was perfect. They only had to remember what had worked and what still needed care. Nadia closed the letter notebook gently, already curious about the next choice. “apartment,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “late” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “reporter” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

時鐘繼續走。娜迪亞雖然疲倦，卻保有完成誠實工作後的好心情。沒有人必須宣稱這天完美；只要記得哪些方法有效、哪些事仍需照顧。娜迪亞輕輕闔上情書筆記本，開始好奇下一個選擇。「apartment。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「late」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「reporter」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

B. The group gives up without speaking to anyone.

**Answer: A**｜正確選項描述本章開頭實際發生的事件。

## CHAPTER 03｜The Letter Desk｜情書代筆桌
*英文正文 841 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Nadia helped her friend June write a note inviting someone to a concert. The college library had its own familiar rhythm. Nadia knew the sound of footsteps there, yet something about this moment called for a closer look. It was tempting to make a quick story out of a small surprise. Instead, Nadia looked for one thing that could be checked and one person who deserved to be heard. There was room in the letter notebook for questions as well as answers, and today the questions were beginning to matter. A little note used “area” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “few” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “night” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘will’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

娜迪亞幫朋友瓊寫字條，邀請心上人去聽音樂會。大學圖書館有自己熟悉的節奏。娜迪亞熟悉那裡的腳步聲，但此刻某件事值得更仔細地看。一個小驚喜很容易讓人立刻編出解釋。娜迪亞卻先尋找一件可核對的事，以及一位需要被傾聽的人。情書筆記本裡不只可以寫答案，也能記下問題；今天，那些問題開始變得重要。一張小筆記以「area」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「few」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「night」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『will』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

The first reaction in the college library was a mixture of surprise and hope. Nadia felt both, and did not pretend otherwise. Eli pointed out that a helpful choice for one person might still make trouble for someone else. Together they tried to find a step small enough to test, but useful enough to show whether their idea worked. Eli did not treat silence as an empty space that needed a clever answer. That made Nadia want to speak in her own words. “all,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “eraser” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “modern” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “traffic” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

大學圖書館裡第一時間既有驚訝，也有希望。娜迪亞兩種感受都有，並不打算假裝沒有。艾利指出，對某人有幫助的選擇，仍可能給另一個人添麻煩。兩人一起尋找一個夠小、能安全測試，同時足以證明想法是否可行的步驟。艾利不把沉默當成必須用聰明答案填滿的空白。這讓娜迪亞想用自己的話說話。「all。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「eraser」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「modern」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「traffic」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

June said the note sounded too grand for her everyday way of speaking. The trouble was real, even if the danger had not yet become clear to everyone in the college library. Nadia took a step back to let someone with more experience speak. Help did not always mean being the first person to act. That gave Eli time to notice a detail that had been lost in the noise. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “email” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “mail,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “quarter” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

瓊說字條太華麗，和自己的說話方式不一樣。麻煩是真實的，即使大學圖書館裡並非每個人都看清了風險。娜迪亞退一步，讓更有經驗的人說話。幫忙不一定代表第一個採取行動。這讓艾利有時間注意到先前被吵鬧掩蓋的細節。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「email」，於是停下來詢問意思。「mail。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「quarter」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

the writing teacher joined the conversation after hearing both sides. The questions were simple, but the answers were not. Who would be affected? What could be checked today? What should wait until someone with the right skill was present? Nadia copied the answers into the letter notebook. A plan became easier to follow when it was written for people besides its maker. “Let us keep ‘define’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “less” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “p.m.,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

寫作老師聽過雙方意見後加入討論。問題很簡單，答案卻未必簡單。誰會受影響？今天能確認什麼？哪些事要等具備專業能力的人在場？娜迪亞把答案抄進情書筆記本。當計畫是寫給所有人看，而不只是制定者自己時，也就更容易執行。「把『define』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「less」，於是停下來詢問意思。「p.m.。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

Nadia asked June to write the first line herself, then made only small changes. The new detail made the room seem a little different. Things that had looked unrelated began to form a line Nadia could follow. Eli warned that one clear clue did not mean they knew everything. Nadia agreed and wrote the date beside the note. There was work still to do, but now they understood why that work mattered. The word “begin” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘leave’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “plant” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

娜迪亞請瓊自己寫第一句，之後只做小修改。新細節讓房間看起來有些不同。原本互不相關的事物，開始連成娜迪亞能循著走的一條線。艾利提醒，一條清楚線索不代表知道全部。娜迪亞同意了，在筆記旁寫下日期。仍有很多事要做，但大家現在知道為什麼這些事重要。「begin」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『leave』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「plant」，於是停下來詢問意思。

**EN**

When Nadia returned to the college library, the place was almost the same. The difference was in what Nadia now understood. Eli had helped without taking away the choice, and that made the help easier to accept. For now, they could rest. Tomorrow would ask its own question. Nadia circled “basketball” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “lamp” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘orange’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

當娜迪亞回到大學圖書館，地方看起來幾乎沒變。真正不同的是娜迪亞的理解。艾利提供幫助，卻沒有奪走選擇權，這份幫助也就更容易被接受。現在他們可以休息。明天自有明天的問題。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「basketball」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「lamp」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『orange』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

## CHAPTER 04｜Rules for a Writer｜寫信人的規則
*英文正文 755 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Eli suggested that Nadia post a clear list of rules beside her letter desk. A small event can change the meaning of an ordinary day. Nadia had been thinking about simple things in the college library when a detail refused to fit the old picture. For a moment, everyone around seemed busy with something else. Nadia tried not to rush, because a wrong guess could make the problem harder for someone nearby. The letter notebook was close at hand, ready for the facts that would be easy to forget once people began talking. The word “bowl” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘knock’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

艾利建議娜迪亞在代筆桌旁張貼清楚規則。一件小事可能改變平凡日子的意義。娜迪亞原本在大學圖書館想著平常的事，卻注意到一個與過去認知不符的細節。那一刻，周圍的人似乎都忙著別的事。娜迪亞不想衝動，因為錯誤猜測可能使身邊的人更為難。情書筆記本就在手邊，可以把眾人開口後容易忘記的事實記下來。「bowl」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『knock』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

For a while, Nadia and Eli stood beside the letter notebook and talked in low voices. Neither wanted a loud argument to take the place of useful work. They tried to say exactly what they knew. When they reached a point they could not explain, they wrote a question mark instead of guessing. Nadia was learning that courage could sound like a quiet request for more information. Nadia circled “block” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “head” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘taxicab’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

娜迪亞與艾利在情書筆記本旁低聲交談了一會兒。兩人都不希望吵架取代有用的行動。他們努力說清自己知道什麼。遇到無法解釋的地方，就畫上問號，而不是隨便猜。娜迪亞漸漸明白，勇敢有時只是安靜地要求更多資訊。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「block」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「head」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『taxicab』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

One rule said she must never send a letter without the real sender's agreement. A mistake would have been easier to hide than to explain. Nadia chose to admit what was still missing instead. Eli did not offer praise just to make the room feel better. Instead, there was a useful question about the next step. It was a smaller answer than the one Nadia wanted, but it was an answer they could trust. A letter belonged to the person who meant it, not the person who could polish it best. Nadia was still learning how to help without taking over. A little note used “bath” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “green” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “surprise” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

其中一條規則是，未經真正寄件人同意絕不寄信。掩蓋錯誤可能比解釋容易，娜迪亞卻選擇承認尚未補齊的部分。艾利沒有為了讓氣氛好些就隨意稱讚，而是提出一個對下一步有用的問題。這個答案比娜迪亞原本想要的小，卻是可以相信的答案。信應該屬於真正有心意的人，而非最會修飾字句的人。娜迪亞仍在學習如何幫忙而不越界。一張小筆記以「bath」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「green」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「surprise」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

The sound of the smell of ink and tea came back as everyone paused. For a minute, Nadia stopped trying to be the person with every answer. Eli asked whether their next choice could be undone if it turned out wrong. That question mattered more than a fast promise. They wrote a careful test on the letter notebook and agreed who would be responsible for it. “east,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “pass” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

眾人停下時，又感覺到墨水與茶的香氣。有一分鐘，娜迪亞不再試著當什麼都懂的人。艾利問，如果下一步選錯了，還能不能改正。這問題比匆忙的承諾更重要。兩人把審慎的測試寫進情書筆記本，並約定誰負責。「east。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「pass」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

Nadia added a new rule: no one could buy a promise about another person's feelings. For a heartbeat, nobody spoke. Nadia looked from Eli to the letter notebook, trying to understand how the new fact changed the plan. The answer was not magic that could remove every risk. It was a reason to change one real action before time ran out. Nadia felt nervous and hopeful at once, and decided to keep both feelings in view. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “cute” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “Mrs.,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

娜迪亞補上一條：沒有人能買到別人的感情承諾。有一瞬間，沒有人說話。娜迪亞看看艾利，又看看情書筆記本，試著弄懂新事實如何改變計畫。答案不是能消除所有風險的魔法，而是提醒大家要在時間結束前改變一項真實行動。娜迪亞同時感到緊張與希望，決定坦然面對兩種感受。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「cute」，於是停下來詢問意思。「Mrs.。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

There was no great speech at the end of the day. Nadia and Eli shared a look that said they had both noticed the change. The next step might be harder, but it would begin with better facts and a little more trust. The letter notebook remained on the table as a quiet reminder that no one had to solve everything alone. “Let us keep ‘cross’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “medium” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

這一天結束時沒有華麗演說。娜迪亞與艾利交換眼神，知道彼此都看見了改變。下一步也許更難，卻能從較清楚的事實與多一點信任開始。情書筆記本靜靜留在桌上，提醒大家沒有人必須獨自解決所有問題。「把『cross』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「medium」，於是停下來詢問意思。

## CHAPTER 05｜An Invitation for Two｜兩個人的邀請
*英文正文 818 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

June invited Nadia and Eli to the college music show so she would not feel alone. Some days begin with a plan; others begin with a question no one had expected. This one gave Nadia both. At the college library, people were coming and going, each carrying a different idea about what was important. The letter notebook made it possible to hold on to a few clear details while the rest of the world kept moving. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “many” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “straight,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “toe” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

瓊邀請娜迪亞與艾利一起看校內音樂表演，免得自己孤單。有些日子從計畫開始，有些日子卻從意料之外的問題開始。這一天，娜迪亞兩者都遇上了。在大學圖書館，人們來來往往，每個人都覺得不同的事最重要。情書筆記本讓娜迪亞在世界不停變動時，仍能抓住幾個清楚的細節。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「many」，於是停下來詢問意思。「straight。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「toe」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

Eli listened without offering an easy answer. That was unusual enough for Nadia to notice. They named the part they understood, then left space for the part that still felt unclear. A good plan did not have to hide its weak points. Someone nearby had a different idea, and the disagreement made the question sharper instead of ending the conversation. “Let us keep ‘feed’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “slow” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “through,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

艾利仔細聆聽，沒有立刻給出簡單答案。娜迪亞注意到這份不同尋常的耐心。他們先說出已了解的部分，再為仍不清楚的地方留空間。好的計畫不需要掩藏弱點。附近有人提出不同想法，這場分歧沒有讓對話結束，反而讓問題更清楚。「把『feed』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「slow」，於是停下來詢問意思。「through。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

Nadia worried people would call Eli her boyfriend just because they sat together. Nadia felt a quick wish to fix everything at once. Then the faces nearby made the choice more complicated. It was not only about the honest letter. Other people had time, hopes, and worries of their own. Eli drew a line across the letter notebook: facts on one side, guesses on the other. That simple mark changed how they spoke. The word “farm” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘sell’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “think” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

娜迪亞擔心兩人坐在一起，別人就會稱艾利是她男友。娜迪亞一瞬間很想把所有事一次解決，但附近每張臉都讓選擇變得複雜。這不只是誠實的信的問題。其他人也有自己的時間、希望與憂慮。艾利在情書筆記本上畫一條線，一側寫事實，另一側寫猜測。這個簡單記號改變了他們說話的方式。「farm」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『sell』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「think」，於是停下來詢問意思。

**EN**

A fresh page in the letter notebook gave the group a way to start again without pretending the past had not happened. Nadia wrote down what could happen next, while Eli named the one thing they still needed to ask. They were not making a perfect picture. They were making a map that another person could read and challenge. A letter belonged to the person who meant it, not the person who could polish it best. Nadia was still learning how to help without taking over. Nadia circled “bank” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “oil” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘teenager’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “yellow” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

情書筆記本的新頁面讓大家重新開始，也不必否認過去發生的事。娜迪亞寫下接下來可能發生的事，艾利則說出仍要詢問的一件事。他們畫的不是完美圖像，而是一張別人也看得懂、也能提出疑問的地圖。信應該屬於真正有心意的人，而非最會修飾字句的人。娜迪亞仍在學習如何幫忙而不越界。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「bank」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「oil」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『teenager』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「yellow」，於是停下來詢問意思。

**EN**

Eli said they could be friends without explaining themselves to the whole room. Once the surprise passed, Nadia wanted to know what the new fact would mean tomorrow. Eli turned back to the letter notebook and crossed out a guess that no longer fitted what they knew. Neither called the mistake foolish. Changing your mind after new evidence was part of learning. A little note used “movie” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “string” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “where” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

艾利說他們可以當朋友，不必向全場解釋。驚喜過後，娜迪亞開始想知道這個新事實明天會帶來什麼改變。艾利重新翻開情書筆記本，劃掉一個已不符合事實的猜測。兩人都沒有說那是愚蠢的錯。依新證據改變想法，本來就是學習的一部分。一張小筆記以「movie」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「string」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「where」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

Later, when the noise of the day grew quiet, Nadia remembered one face more clearly than any sign or number. The work had not been easy, and the result did not belong to one person. Still, something had moved forward. Before putting away the letter notebook, Nadia wrote one question for the next day. It was not the end of the story yet. “moon,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “strange” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “topic” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

後來，白天的吵鬧安靜下來時，娜迪亞記得最清楚的是一張臉，而不是任何標誌或數字。工作並不容易，成果也不屬於單一個人。然而，有些事情確實向前走了。收起情書筆記本前，娜迪亞寫下一個留給明天的問題。故事還沒有結束。「moon。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「strange」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「topic」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

## CHAPTER 06｜A Letter Nobody Sends｜不會寄出的信
*英文正文 807 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Nadia started a letter to herself about the kind of person she hoped to become. Before anyone called the day strange, Nadia had noticed how quiet the college library could become when people were worried. The smallest sounds seemed clear, and the smell of ink and tea made the place feel both familiar and new. Rather than act as if every answer was obvious, Nadia opened the letter notebook and began with what had really happened. A little note used “do” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “however” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “speak” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

娜迪亞開始寫一封給自己的信，談她希望成為什麼樣的人。在人們說這一天奇怪之前，娜迪亞就注意到，當大家擔心時，大學圖書館會變得多麼安靜。細微的聲音變得清楚，墨水與茶的香氣也讓這地方既熟悉又陌生。娜迪亞沒有裝作一切都很明白，而是打開情書筆記本，從真正發生的事情開始。一張小筆記以「do」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「however」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「speak」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

Nobody could say yet how the story would end. Nadia found this uncomfortable, especially with the honest letter still waiting for an answer. Eli suggested they take turns speaking, because repeating the same fear would not create a new solution. A little order returned to the conversation, and that made the next difficult step possible. “desk,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “history” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “shop” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

沒有人知道事情會如何結束。娜迪亞覺得不安，尤其誠實的信還等著答案。艾利建議輪流發言，因為一再重複恐懼並不會帶來新方法。談話恢復了一些秩序，也讓接下來的難題變得有機會處理。「desk。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「history」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「shop」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

**EN**

The page remained almost empty because she was used to borrowing other people's wishes. This was the point where a simple wish met the world as it really was. Nadia wanted to help, but wanting did not make every road safe or every rule fair. Eli asked what the other people involved might lose if the wrong choice were made. The answer took longer than either had expected. They agreed not to build the rest of their plan on a fact they had never checked. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “deep” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “funny,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “seat” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

紙張幾乎空白，因為她習慣借用別人的願望。這正是簡單願望遇上真實世界的時刻。娜迪亞想幫忙，但只靠想法不會讓所有路變安全，也不會讓所有規則公平。艾利問，如果選錯了，牽涉其中的人可能失去什麼。兩人花了比預期更長的時間才回答。他們同意，不把整個計畫建立在尚未核實的事實上。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「deep」，於是停下來詢問意思。「funny。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「seat」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

The group returned to the letter notebook and looked at the first line again. It now seemed to ask a different question. Nadia asked Eli to explain the idea in plain words, as if telling it to someone who had just arrived. When the idea sounded confusing, they changed the plan rather than blaming the listener. “Let us keep ‘convenient’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “fish” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “rise,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

大家重新看著情書筆記本的第一行，發現它彷彿提出了另一個問題。娜迪亞請艾利用簡單的話解釋，就像對剛來的人說明。說明若讓人困惑，他們就修改計畫，而不是責怪聽的人。「把『convenient』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「fish」，於是停下來詢問意思。「rise。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

Eli offered to listen without telling her what she ought to feel. The answer brought relief, but it also carried a new responsibility. Nadia did not look away from that part. Eli asked who should hear the news next, and whether it belonged to them to share it. They chose the careful answer over the exciting one, though excitement was still in the air. Nadia had learned that a beautiful sentence could be easy to write and hard to mean. Eli never asked her to hide that difference. The word “arm” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘find’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “mud” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “welcome,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

艾利表示願意傾聽，不會替她決定該有什麼感受。答案帶來安心，也帶來新的責任。娜迪亞沒有迴避這一部分。艾利問下一個該讓誰知道消息，以及他們是否有權公開。他們選擇審慎的回答，而非最刺激的那個，雖然空氣中仍充滿興奮。娜迪亞學到，漂亮句子容易寫，真正說到做到卻不容易。艾利從不要求她掩藏差別。「arm」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『find』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「mud」，於是停下來詢問意思。「welcome。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

The clock moved on. Nadia felt tired, yet there was a good kind of energy left after honest work. Nobody had to say that the day was perfect. They only had to remember what had worked and what still needed care. Nadia closed the letter notebook gently, already curious about the next choice. Nadia circled “envelope” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “may” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘surprised’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

時鐘繼續走。娜迪亞雖然疲倦，卻保有完成誠實工作後的好心情。沒有人必須宣稱這天完美；只要記得哪些方法有效、哪些事仍需照顧。娜迪亞輕輕闔上情書筆記本，開始好奇下一個選擇。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「envelope」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「may」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『surprised』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

## CHAPTER 07｜The Lost Blue Envelope｜遺失的藍信封
*英文正文 719 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

A blue envelope vanished from the letter desk before its sender had approved the final words. The college library had its own familiar rhythm. Nadia knew the sound of footsteps there, yet something about this moment called for a closer look. It was tempting to make a quick story out of a small surprise. Instead, Nadia looked for one thing that could be checked and one person who deserved to be heard. There was room in the letter notebook for questions as well as answers, and today the questions were beginning to matter. The word “flower” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘rabbit’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

寄件人尚未確認最終內容，藍色信封就從代筆桌不見了。大學圖書館有自己熟悉的節奏。娜迪亞熟悉那裡的腳步聲，但此刻某件事值得更仔細地看。一個小驚喜很容易讓人立刻編出解釋。娜迪亞卻先尋找一件可核對的事，以及一位需要被傾聽的人。情書筆記本裡不只可以寫答案，也能記下問題；今天，那些問題開始變得重要。「flower」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『rabbit』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

The first reaction in the college library was a mixture of surprise and hope. Nadia felt both, and did not pretend otherwise. Eli pointed out that a helpful choice for one person might still make trouble for someone else. Together they tried to find a step small enough to test, but useful enough to show whether their idea worked. Nadia circled “course” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “popular” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

大學圖書館裡第一時間既有驚訝，也有希望。娜迪亞兩種感受都有，並不打算假裝沒有。艾利指出，對某人有幫助的選擇，仍可能給另一個人添麻煩。兩人一起尋找一個夠小、能安全測試，同時足以證明想法是否可行的步驟。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「course」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「popular」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

Nadia feared someone would send a private draft as though it were finished. The trouble was real, even if the danger had not yet become clear to everyone in the college library. Nadia took a step back to let someone with more experience speak. Help did not always mean being the first person to act. That gave Eli time to notice a detail that had been lost in the noise. A little note used “collect” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “main” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

娜迪亞擔心有人會把私人草稿當成定稿寄出。麻煩是真實的，即使大學圖書館裡並非每個人都看清了風險。娜迪亞退一步，讓更有經驗的人說話。幫忙不一定代表第一個採取行動。這讓艾利有時間注意到先前被吵鬧掩蓋的細節。一張小筆記以「collect」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「main」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

**EN**

the writing teacher joined the conversation after hearing both sides. The questions were simple, but the answers were not. Who would be affected? What could be checked today? What should wait until someone with the right skill was present? Nadia copied the answers into the letter notebook. A plan became easier to follow when it was written for people besides its maker. “class,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “large” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

寫作老師聽過雙方意見後加入討論。問題很簡單，答案卻未必簡單。誰會受影響？今天能確認什麼？哪些事要等具備專業能力的人在場？娜迪亞把答案抄進情書筆記本。當計畫是寫給所有人看，而不只是制定者自己時，也就更容易執行。「class。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「large」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

Eli helped her make a list of who had used the room that afternoon. The new detail made the room seem a little different. Things that had looked unrelated began to form a line Nadia could follow. Eli warned that one clear clue did not mean they knew everything. Nadia agreed and wrote the date beside the note. There was work still to do, but now they understood why that work mattered. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “chocolate” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “housewife,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “west” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

艾利陪她列出下午進出房間的人。新細節讓房間看起來有些不同。原本互不相關的事物，開始連成娜迪亞能循著走的一條線。艾利提醒，一條清楚線索不代表知道全部。娜迪亞同意了，在筆記旁寫下日期。仍有很多事要做，但大家現在知道為什麼這些事重要。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「chocolate」，於是停下來詢問意思。「housewife。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「west」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

When Nadia returned to the college library, the place was almost the same. The difference was in what Nadia now understood. Eli had helped without taking away the choice, and that made the help easier to accept. For now, they could rest. Tomorrow would ask its own question. Nadia had learned that a beautiful sentence could be easy to write and hard to mean. Eli never asked her to hide that difference. “Let us keep ‘by’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “hour” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “start,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

當娜迪亞回到大學圖書館，地方看起來幾乎沒變。真正不同的是娜迪亞的理解。艾利提供幫助，卻沒有奪走選擇權，這份幫助也就更容易被接受。現在他們可以休息。明天自有明天的問題。娜迪亞學到，漂亮句子容易寫，真正說到做到卻不容易。艾利從不要求她掩藏差別。「把『by』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「hour」，於是停下來詢問意思。「start。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

## CHAPTER 08｜The Wrong Name｜錯誤的署名
*英文正文 825 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

A copy of the blue letter appeared on a public college page under the wrong name. A small event can change the meaning of an ordinary day. Nadia had been thinking about simple things in the college library when a detail refused to fit the old picture. For a moment, everyone around seemed busy with something else. Nadia tried not to rush, because a wrong guess could make the problem harder for someone nearby. The letter notebook was close at hand, ready for the facts that would be easy to forget once people began talking. Eli did not treat silence as an empty space that needed a clever answer. That made Nadia want to speak in her own words. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “book” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “fork,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “queen” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

藍色信件的影本出現在校內公開頁面，署名竟是錯的。一件小事可能改變平凡日子的意義。娜迪亞原本在大學圖書館想著平常的事，卻注意到一個與過去認知不符的細節。那一刻，周圍的人似乎都忙著別的事。娜迪亞不想衝動，因為錯誤猜測可能使身邊的人更為難。情書筆記本就在手邊，可以把眾人開口後容易忘記的事實記下來。艾利不把沉默當成必須用聰明答案填滿的空白。這讓娜迪亞想用自己的話說話。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「book」，於是停下來詢問意思。「fork。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「queen」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

For a while, Nadia and Eli stood beside the letter notebook and talked in low voices. Neither wanted a loud argument to take the place of useful work. They tried to say exactly what they knew. When they reached a point they could not explain, they wrote a question mark instead of guessing. Nadia was learning that courage could sound like a quiet request for more information. “Let us keep ‘file’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “noon” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “you,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

娜迪亞與艾利在情書筆記本旁低聲交談了一會兒。兩人都不希望吵架取代有用的行動。他們努力說清自己知道什麼。遇到無法解釋的地方，就畫上問號，而不是隨便猜。娜迪亞漸漸明白，勇敢有時只是安靜地要求更多資訊。「把『file』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「noon」，於是停下來詢問意思。「you。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

Students guessed about two classmates, and Nadia asked them not to spread private claims. A mistake would have been easier to hide than to explain. Nadia chose to admit what was still missing instead. Eli did not offer praise just to make the room feel better. Instead, there was a useful question about the next step. It was a smaller answer than the one Nadia wanted, but it was an answer they could trust. The word “each” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘museum’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “train” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

學生們猜測兩名同學的關係，娜迪亞請大家別傳播私人猜想。掩蓋錯誤可能比解釋容易，娜迪亞卻選擇承認尚未補齊的部分。艾利沒有為了讓氣氛好些就隨意稱讚，而是提出一個對下一步有用的問題。這個答案比娜迪亞原本想要的小，卻是可以相信的答案。「each」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『museum』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「train」，於是停下來詢問意思。

**EN**

The sound of the smell of ink and tea came back as everyone paused. For a minute, Nadia stopped trying to be the person with every answer. Eli asked whether their next choice could be undone if it turned out wrong. That question mattered more than a fast promise. They wrote a careful test on the letter notebook and agreed who would be responsible for it. Nadia circled “dollar” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. Nadia chose to listen before giving advice. “Let us keep ‘tell’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

眾人停下時，又感覺到墨水與茶的香氣。有一分鐘，娜迪亞不再試著當什麼都懂的人。艾利問，如果下一步選錯了，還能不能改正。這問題比匆忙的承諾更重要。兩人把審慎的測試寫進情書筆記本，並約定誰負責。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「dollar」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。娜迪亞選擇先傾聽，再給建議。「把『tell』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

The college office agreed to remove the post while checking who had uploaded it. For a heartbeat, nobody spoke. Nadia looked from Eli to the letter notebook, trying to understand how the new fact changed the plan. The answer was not magic that could remove every risk. It was a reason to change one real action before time ran out. Nadia felt nervous and hopeful at once, and decided to keep both feelings in view. A little note used “dangerous” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “idea” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “successful” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

校方同意在調查上傳者時先移除貼文。有一瞬間，沒有人說話。娜迪亞看看艾利，又看看情書筆記本，試著弄懂新事實如何改變計畫。答案不是能消除所有風險的魔法，而是提醒大家要在時間結束前改變一項真實行動。娜迪亞同時感到緊張與希望，決定坦然面對兩種感受。一張小筆記以「dangerous」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「idea」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「successful」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

There was no great speech at the end of the day. Nadia and Eli shared a look that said they had both noticed the change. The next step might be harder, but it would begin with better facts and a little more trust. The letter notebook remained on the table as a quiet reminder that no one had to solve everything alone. “boring,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “full” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “roll” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

這一天結束時沒有華麗演說。娜迪亞與艾利交換眼神，知道彼此都看見了改變。下一步也許更難，卻能從較清楚的事實與多一點信任開始。情書筆記本靜靜留在桌上，提醒大家沒有人必須獨自解決所有問題。「boring。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「full」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「roll」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

### 理解測驗

**Q1. What happens near the beginning of Chapter 08?**
A. A copy of the blue letter appeared on a public college page under the wrong name.
B. The group gives up without speaking to anyone.
C. Someone wins a prize that has nothing to do with the problem.
**Answer: A**｜正確選項描述本章開頭實際發生的事件。

**Q2. What new fact or choice appears near the end of Chapter 08?**
A. Someone wins a prize that has nothing to do with the problem.
B. The group gives up without speaking to anyone.
C. The college office agreed to remove the post while checking who had uploaded it.
**Answer: C**｜正確選項對應本章結尾已明確寫出的發展。


## CHAPTER 09｜A Quiet Apology｜安靜的道歉
*英文正文 809 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Nadia apologized to June for keeping unfinished drafts in an open box. Some days begin with a plan; others begin with a question no one had expected. This one gave Nadia both. At the college library, people were coming and going, each carrying a different idea about what was important. The letter notebook made it possible to hold on to a few clear details while the rest of the world kept moving. A little note used “brave” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “meat” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “singer” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

娜迪亞向瓊道歉，因為她把未完成草稿放在開放的箱子裡。有些日子從計畫開始，有些日子卻從意料之外的問題開始。這一天，娜迪亞兩者都遇上了。在大學圖書館，人們來來往往，每個人都覺得不同的事最重要。情書筆記本讓娜迪亞在世界不停變動時，仍能抓住幾個清楚的細節。一張小筆記以「brave」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「meat」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「singer」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

Eli listened without offering an easy answer. That was unusual enough for Nadia to notice. They named the part they understood, then left space for the part that still felt unclear. A good plan did not have to hide its weak points. Someone nearby had a different idea, and the disagreement made the question sharper instead of ending the conversation. Eli did not treat silence as an empty space that needed a clever answer. That made Nadia want to speak in her own words. “bear,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “lose” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “sad” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “whose” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

艾利仔細聆聽，沒有立刻給出簡單答案。娜迪亞注意到這份不同尋常的耐心。他們先說出已了解的部分，再為仍不清楚的地方留空間。好的計畫不需要掩藏弱點。附近有人提出不同想法，這場分歧沒有讓對話結束，反而讓問題更清楚。艾利不把沉默當成必須用聰明答案填滿的空白。這讓娜迪亞想用自己的話說話。「bear。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「lose」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「sad」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「whose」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

June said the problem was not only the letter but that strangers were deciding her feelings. Nadia felt a quick wish to fix everything at once. Then the faces nearby made the choice more complicated. It was not only about the honest letter. Other people had time, hopes, and worries of their own. Eli drew a line across the letter notebook: facts on one side, guesses on the other. That simple mark changed how they spoke. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “lonely” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “ring,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “well” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

瓊說問題不只是信，而是陌生人替她決定感情。娜迪亞一瞬間很想把所有事一次解決，但附近每張臉都讓選擇變得複雜。這不只是誠實的信的問題。其他人也有自己的時間、希望與憂慮。艾利在情書筆記本上畫一條線，一側寫事實，另一側寫猜測。這個簡單記號改變了他們說話的方式。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「lonely」，於是停下來詢問意思。「ring。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「well」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

A fresh page in the letter notebook gave the group a way to start again without pretending the past had not happened. Nadia wrote down what could happen next, while Eli named the one thing they still needed to ask. They were not making a perfect picture. They were making a map that another person could read and challenge. “Let us keep ‘keep’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “photograph” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “warm,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

情書筆記本的新頁面讓大家重新開始，也不必否認過去發生的事。娜迪亞寫下接下來可能發生的事，艾利則說出仍要詢問的一件事。他們畫的不是完美圖像，而是一張別人也看得懂、也能提出疑問的地圖。「把『keep』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「photograph」，於是停下來詢問意思。「warm。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

Together they wrote a short public correction without sharing private details. Once the surprise passed, Nadia wanted to know what the new fact would mean tomorrow. Eli turned back to the letter notebook and crossed out a guess that no longer fitted what they knew. Neither called the mistake foolish. Changing your mind after new evidence was part of learning. The word “foreigner” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘nothing’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “wall” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

她們一起寫簡短澄清，不公開私人細節。驚喜過後，娜迪亞開始想知道這個新事實明天會帶來什麼改變。艾利重新翻開情書筆記本，劃掉一個已不符合事實的猜測。兩人都沒有說那是愚蠢的錯。依新證據改變想法，本來就是學習的一部分。「foreigner」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『nothing』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「wall」，於是停下來詢問意思。

**EN**

Later, when the noise of the day grew quiet, Nadia remembered one face more clearly than any sign or number. The work had not been easy, and the result did not belong to one person. Still, something had moved forward. Before putting away the letter notebook, Nadia wrote one question for the next day. It was not the end of the story yet. Nadia circled “farmer” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “milk” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘toilet’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

後來，白天的吵鬧安靜下來時，娜迪亞記得最清楚的是一張臉，而不是任何標誌或數字。工作並不容易，成果也不屬於單一個人。然而，有些事情確實向前走了。收起情書筆記本前，娜迪亞寫下一個留給明天的問題。故事還沒有結束。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「farmer」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「milk」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『toilet』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

## CHAPTER 10｜The Copy That Hurt｜傷人的影本
*英文正文 818 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

The college found that someone had copied Nadia's best-known letter and changed its name to make two friends quarrel. Before anyone called the day strange, Nadia had noticed how quiet the college library could become when people were worried. The smallest sounds seemed clear, and the smell of ink and tea made the place feel both familiar and new. Rather than act as if every answer was obvious, Nadia opened the letter notebook and began with what had really happened. The word “dark” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘potato’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “sweet” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

學校查出有人複製娜迪亞最有名的情書，換上別人的名字，讓兩位朋友吵架。在人們說這一天奇怪之前，娜迪亞就注意到，當大家擔心時，大學圖書館會變得多麼安靜。細微的聲音變得清楚，墨水與茶的香氣也讓這地方既熟悉又陌生。娜迪亞沒有裝作一切都很明白，而是打開情書筆記本，從真正發生的事情開始。「dark」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『potato』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「sweet」，於是停下來詢問意思。

**EN**

Nobody could say yet how the story would end. Nadia found this uncomfortable, especially with the honest letter still waiting for an answer. Eli suggested they take turns speaking, because repeating the same fear would not create a new solution. A little order returned to the conversation, and that made the next difficult step possible. Nadia circled “button” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “menu” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘student’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

沒有人知道事情會如何結束。娜迪亞覺得不安，尤其誠實的信還等著答案。艾利建議輪流發言，因為一再重複恐懼並不會帶來新方法。談話恢復了一些秩序，也讓接下來的難題變得有機會處理。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「button」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「menu」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『student』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

Nadia realized that beautiful words could be used badly when the true sender disappeared. This was the point where a simple wish met the world as it really was. Nadia wanted to help, but wanting did not make every road safe or every rule fair. Eli asked what the other people involved might lose if the wrong choice were made. The answer took longer than either had expected. They agreed not to build the rest of their plan on a fact they had never checked. A letter belonged to the person who meant it, not the person who could polish it best. Nadia was still learning how to help without taking over. A little note used “around” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “interesting” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “stay” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘worry’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

娜迪亞明白，再美的文字在失去真正寄件人後也可能被利用。這正是簡單願望遇上真實世界的時刻。娜迪亞想幫忙，但只靠想法不會讓所有路變安全，也不會讓所有規則公平。艾利問，如果選錯了，牽涉其中的人可能失去什麼。兩人花了比預期更長的時間才回答。他們同意，不把整個計畫建立在尚未核實的事實上。信應該屬於真正有心意的人，而非最會修飾字句的人。娜迪亞仍在學習如何幫忙而不越界。一張小筆記以「around」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「interesting」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「stay」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『worry』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

The group returned to the letter notebook and looked at the first line again. It now seemed to ask a different question. Nadia asked Eli to explain the idea in plain words, as if telling it to someone who had just arrived. When the idea sounded confusing, they changed the plan rather than blaming the listener. “have,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “sound” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “watch” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

大家重新看著情書筆記本的第一行，發現它彷彿提出了另一個問題。娜迪亞請艾利用簡單的話解釋，就像對剛來的人說明。說明若讓人困惑，他們就修改計畫，而不是責怪聽的人。「have。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「sound」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「watch」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

**EN**

She closed her letter desk until she could make a safer way to work. The answer brought relief, but it also carried a new responsibility. Nadia did not look away from that part. Eli asked who should hear the news next, and whether it belonged to them to share it. They chose the careful answer over the exciting one, though excitement was still in the air. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “fall” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “smile,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “usually” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

她暫時關閉代筆桌，決心想出更安全的方法。答案帶來安心，也帶來新的責任。娜迪亞沒有迴避這一部分。艾利問下一個該讓誰知道消息，以及他們是否有權公開。他們選擇審慎的回答，而非最刺激的那個，雖然空氣中仍充滿興奮。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「fall」，於是停下來詢問意思。「smile。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「usually」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

The clock moved on. Nadia felt tired, yet there was a good kind of energy left after honest work. Nobody had to say that the day was perfect. They only had to remember what had worked and what still needed care. Nadia closed the letter notebook gently, already curious about the next choice. “Let us keep ‘evening’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “rose” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “trouble,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

時鐘繼續走。娜迪亞雖然疲倦，卻保有完成誠實工作後的好心情。沒有人必須宣稱這天完美；只要記得哪些方法有效、哪些事仍需照顧。娜迪亞輕輕闔上情書筆記本，開始好奇下一個選擇。「把『evening』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「rose」，於是停下來詢問意思。「trouble。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

## CHAPTER 11｜Who Owns a Sentence｜一句話屬於誰
*英文正文 780 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Nadia asked the college writing teacher how to protect a person's own voice. The college library had its own familiar rhythm. Nadia knew the sound of footsteps there, yet something about this moment called for a closer look. It was tempting to make a quick story out of a small surprise. Instead, Nadia looked for one thing that could be checked and one person who deserved to be heard. There was room in the letter notebook for questions as well as answers, and today the questions were beginning to matter. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “color” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “kite,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “those” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

娜迪亞詢問寫作老師，如何保護一個人自己的聲音。大學圖書館有自己熟悉的節奏。娜迪亞熟悉那裡的腳步聲，但此刻某件事值得更仔細地看。一個小驚喜很容易讓人立刻編出解釋。娜迪亞卻先尋找一件可核對的事，以及一位需要被傾聽的人。情書筆記本裡不只可以寫答案，也能記下問題；今天，那些問題開始變得重要。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「color」，於是停下來詢問意思。「kite。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「those」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

The first reaction in the college library was a mixture of surprise and hope. Nadia felt both, and did not pretend otherwise. Eli pointed out that a helpful choice for one person might still make trouble for someone else. Together they tried to find a step small enough to test, but useful enough to show whether their idea worked. “Let us keep ‘check’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “joke” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “thin,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

大學圖書館裡第一時間既有驚訝，也有希望。娜迪亞兩種感受都有，並不打算假裝沒有。艾利指出，對某人有幫助的選擇，仍可能給另一個人添麻煩。兩人一起尋找一個夠小、能安全測試，同時足以證明想法是否可行的步驟。「把『check』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「joke」，於是停下來詢問意思。「thin。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

The teacher said helping was different from speaking in someone else's place. The trouble was real, even if the danger had not yet become clear to everyone in the college library. Nadia took a step back to let someone with more experience speak. Help did not always mean being the first person to act. That gave Eli time to notice a detail that had been lost in the noise. The word “belt” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘hospital’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “send” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant.

**繁中**

老師說，幫忙和替別人發言是兩件事。麻煩是真實的，即使大學圖書館裡並非每個人都看清了風險。娜迪亞退一步，讓更有經驗的人說話。幫忙不一定代表第一個採取行動。這讓艾利有時間注意到先前被吵鬧掩蓋的細節。「belt」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『hospital』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「send」，於是停下來詢問意思。

**EN**

the writing teacher joined the conversation after hearing both sides. The questions were simple, but the answers were not. Who would be affected? What could be checked today? What should wait until someone with the right skill was present? Nadia copied the answers into the letter notebook. A plan became easier to follow when it was written for people besides its maker. A letter belonged to the person who meant it, not the person who could polish it best. Nadia was still learning how to help without taking over. Nadia circled “basket” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “health” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘player’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

寫作老師聽過雙方意見後加入討論。問題很簡單，答案卻未必簡單。誰會受影響？今天能確認什麼？哪些事要等具備專業能力的人在場？娜迪亞把答案抄進情書筆記本。當計畫是寫給所有人看，而不只是制定者自己時，也就更容易執行。信應該屬於真正有心意的人，而非最會修飾字句的人。娜迪亞仍在學習如何幫忙而不越界。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「basket」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「health」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『player』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

Nadia designed a form where each sender approved the exact final letter. The new detail made the room seem a little different. Things that had looked unrelated began to form a line Nadia could follow. Eli warned that one clear clue did not mean they knew everything. Nadia agreed and wrote the date beside the note. There was work still to do, but now they understood why that work mattered. A little note used “hate” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “picture” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

娜迪亞設計表格，讓每位寄件人確認信件的完整內容。新細節讓房間看起來有些不同。原本互不相關的事物，開始連成娜迪亞能循著走的一條線。艾利提醒，一條清楚線索不代表知道全部。娜迪亞同意了，在筆記旁寫下日期。仍有很多事要做，但大家現在知道為什麼這些事重要。一張小筆記以「hate」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「picture」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

**EN**

When Nadia returned to the college library, the place was almost the same. The difference was in what Nadia now understood. Eli had helped without taking away the choice, and that made the help easier to accept. For now, they could rest. Tomorrow would ask its own question. “dear,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “often” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “white” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

當娜迪亞回到大學圖書館，地方看起來幾乎沒變。真正不同的是娜迪亞的理解。艾利提供幫助，卻沒有奪走選擇權，這份幫助也就更容易被接受。現在他們可以休息。明天自有明天的問題。「dear。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「often」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「white」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

## CHAPTER 12｜The New Table｜新桌子
*英文正文 853 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Nadia reopened the desk as a writing workshop where visitors made their own first drafts. A small event can change the meaning of an ordinary day. Nadia had been thinking about simple things in the college library when a detail refused to fit the old picture. For a moment, everyone around seemed busy with something else. Nadia tried not to rush, because a wrong guess could make the problem harder for someone nearby. The letter notebook was close at hand, ready for the facts that would be easy to forget once people began talking. A little note used “corner” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “media” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “supermarket” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

娜迪亞把代筆桌改成寫作工坊，來訪者必須先寫自己的初稿。一件小事可能改變平凡日子的意義。娜迪亞原本在大學圖書館想著平常的事，卻注意到一個與過去認知不符的細節。那一刻，周圍的人似乎都忙著別的事。娜迪亞不想衝動，因為錯誤猜測可能使身邊的人更為難。情書筆記本就在手邊，可以把眾人開口後容易忘記的事實記下來。一張小筆記以「corner」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「media」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「supermarket」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

For a while, Nadia and Eli stood beside the letter notebook and talked in low voices. Neither wanted a loud argument to take the place of useful work. They tried to say exactly what they knew. When they reached a point they could not explain, they wrote a question mark instead of guessing. Nadia was learning that courage could sound like a quiet request for more information. “celebrate,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “last” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “poor” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

娜迪亞與艾利在情書筆記本旁低聲交談了一會兒。兩人都不希望吵架取代有用的行動。他們努力說清自己知道什麼。遇到無法解釋的地方，就畫上問號，而不是隨便猜。娜迪亞漸漸明白，勇敢有時只是安靜地要求更多資訊。「celebrate。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「last」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「poor」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

**EN**

Fewer students came, and she worried the new plan was a failure. A mistake would have been easier to hide than to explain. Nadia chose to admit what was still missing instead. Eli did not offer praise just to make the room feel better. Instead, there was a useful question about the next step. It was a smaller answer than the one Nadia wanted, but it was an answer they could trust. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “band” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “key,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “play” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

來的人變少，她擔心新做法失敗了。掩蓋錯誤可能比解釋容易，娜迪亞卻選擇承認尚未補齊的部分。艾利沒有為了讓氣氛好些就隨意稱讚，而是提出一個對下一步有用的問題。這個答案比娜迪亞原本想要的小，卻是可以相信的答案。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「band」，於是停下來詢問意思。「key。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「play」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

The sound of the smell of ink and tea came back as everyone paused. For a minute, Nadia stopped trying to be the person with every answer. Eli asked whether their next choice could be undone if it turned out wrong. That question mattered more than a fast promise. They wrote a careful test on the letter notebook and agreed who would be responsible for it. “Let us keep ‘afternoon’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “I” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “package,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

眾人停下時，又感覺到墨水與茶的香氣。有一分鐘，娜迪亞不再試著當什麼都懂的人。艾利問，如果下一步選錯了，還能不能改正。這問題比匆忙的承諾更重要。兩人把審慎的測試寫進情書筆記本，並約定誰負責。「把『afternoon』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「I」，於是停下來詢問意思。「package。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

June said one honest letter was worth more than many perfect borrowed ones. For a heartbeat, nobody spoke. Nadia looked from Eli to the letter notebook, trying to understand how the new fact changed the plan. The answer was not magic that could remove every risk. It was a reason to change one real action before time ran out. Nadia felt nervous and hopeful at once, and decided to keep both feelings in view. Nadia had learned that a beautiful sentence could be easy to write and hard to mean. Eli never asked her to hide that difference. The word “actor” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘glasses’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “or” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “tired,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

瓊說，一封誠實的信比許多借來的完美情書更有價值。有一瞬間，沒有人說話。娜迪亞看看艾利，又看看情書筆記本，試著弄懂新事實如何改變計畫。答案不是能消除所有風險的魔法，而是提醒大家要在時間結束前改變一項真實行動。娜迪亞同時感到緊張與希望，決定坦然面對兩種感受。娜迪亞學到，漂亮句子容易寫，真正說到做到卻不容易。艾利從不要求她掩藏差別。「actor」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『glasses』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「or」，於是停下來詢問意思。「tired。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。

**EN**

There was no great speech at the end of the day. Nadia and Eli shared a look that said they had both noticed the change. The next step might be harder, but it would begin with better facts and a little more trust. The letter notebook remained on the table as a quiet reminder that no one had to solve everything alone. Nadia circled “die” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “o’clock” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘ticket’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

這一天結束時沒有華麗演說。娜迪亞與艾利交換眼神，知道彼此都看見了改變。下一步也許更難，卻能從較清楚的事實與多一點信任開始。情書筆記本靜靜留在桌上，提醒大家沒有人必須獨自解決所有問題。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「die」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「o’clock」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『ticket』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

## CHAPTER 13｜A Walk Without Paper｜沒有紙張的散步
*英文正文 763 詞；英中對照 6 組；本章新目標詞 20 條*

### 正文與翻譯

**EN**

Eli asked Nadia to walk beside the river without bringing her notebook. Some days begin with a plan; others begin with a question no one had expected. This one gave Nadia both. At the college library, people were coming and going, each carrying a different idea about what was important. The letter notebook made it possible to hold on to a few clear details while the rest of the world kept moving. The word “high” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘soldier’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

艾利邀娜迪亞沿河散步，請她不要帶筆記本。有些日子從計畫開始，有些日子卻從意料之外的問題開始。這一天，娜迪亞兩者都遇上了。在大學圖書館，人們來來往往，每個人都覺得不同的事最重要。情書筆記本讓娜迪亞在世界不停變動時，仍能抓住幾個清楚的細節。「high」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『soldier』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

Eli listened without offering an easy answer. That was unusual enough for Nadia to notice. They named the part they understood, then left space for the part that still felt unclear. A good plan did not have to hide its weak points. Someone nearby had a different idea, and the disagreement made the question sharper instead of ending the conversation. Nadia circled “fight” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “ride” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation. “Let us keep ‘window’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.”

**繁中**

艾利仔細聆聽，沒有立刻給出簡單答案。娜迪亞注意到這份不同尋常的耐心。他們先說出已了解的部分，再為仍不清楚的地方留空間。好的計畫不需要掩藏弱點。附近有人提出不同想法，這場分歧沒有讓對話結束，反而讓問題更清楚。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「fight」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「ride」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。「把『window』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」

**EN**

She kept turning simple feelings into lines she might use for another letter. Nadia felt a quick wish to fix everything at once. Then the faces nearby made the choice more complicated. It was not only about the honest letter. Other people had time, hopes, and worries of their own. Eli drew a line across the letter notebook: facts on one side, guesses on the other. That simple mark changed how they spoke. A little note used “duck” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “rain” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence. The word “wide” appeared in an example someone had written earlier, and Eli asked if the example fit their situation.

**繁中**

她仍不斷把簡單感受轉成將來可用的句子。娜迪亞一瞬間很想把所有事一次解決，但附近每張臉都讓選擇變得複雜。這不只是誠實的信的問題。其他人也有自己的時間、希望與憂慮。艾利在情書筆記本上畫一條線，一側寫事實，另一側寫猜測。這個簡單記號改變了他們說話的方式。一張小筆記以「duck」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「rain」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。「wide」出現在前人留下的例句裡，艾利問這個例子是否適合現在的情況。

**EN**

A fresh page in the letter notebook gave the group a way to start again without pretending the past had not happened. Nadia wrote down what could happen next, while Eli named the one thing they still needed to ask. They were not making a perfect picture. They were making a map that another person could read and challenge. “chair,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “member” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful. Nadia circled “together” on a reading sheet, not to win a test but to remember the meaning of the whole sentence.

**繁中**

情書筆記本的新頁面讓大家重新開始，也不必否認過去發生的事。娜迪亞寫下接下來可能發生的事，艾利則說出仍要詢問的一件事。他們畫的不是完美圖像，而是一張別人也看得懂、也能提出疑問的地圖。「chair。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「member」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。娜迪亞在閱讀單上圈出「together」，不是為了考高分，而是為了理解整句話。

**EN**

When Eli asked what she wanted, she finally answered: another walk. Once the surprise passed, Nadia wanted to know what the new fact would mean tomorrow. Eli turned back to the letter notebook and crossed out a guess that no longer fitted what they knew. Neither called the mistake foolish. Changing your mind after new evidence was part of learning. In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “camp” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “lead,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see. A little note used “though” as its key word; Nadia kept the note because the rest of the line was still useful.

**繁中**

艾利問她真正想要什麼，她終於回答：「再散步一次。」驚喜過後，娜迪亞開始想知道這個新事實明天會帶來什麼改變。艾利重新翻開情書筆記本，劃掉一個已不符合事實的猜測。兩人都沒有說那是愚蠢的錯。依新證據改變想法，本來就是學習的一部分。在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「camp」，於是停下來詢問意思。「lead。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。一張小筆記以「though」為關鍵字；娜迪亞留下它，因為剩下的句子仍然有用。

**EN**

Later, when the noise of the day grew quiet, Nadia remembered one face more clearly than any sign or number. The work had not been easy, and the result did not belong to one person. Still, something had moved forward. Before putting away the letter notebook, Nadia wrote one question for the next day. It was not the end of the story yet. Nadia had learned that a beautiful sentence could be easy to write and hard to mean. Eli never asked her to hide that difference. “Let us keep ‘brown’ in our word notes,” Nadia said. “We can check its meaning again if we need it.” In the corner of the letter notebook, Nadia found the word “honey” beside an old drawing and stopped to ask what it meant. “stair,” Eli read from the practice page, then tried to connect the word to something they could actually see.

**繁中**

後來，白天的吵鬧安靜下來時，娜迪亞記得最清楚的是一張臉，而不是任何標誌或數字。工作並不容易，成果也不屬於單一個人。然而，有些事情確實向前走了。收起情書筆記本前，娜迪亞寫下一個留給明天的問題。故事還沒有結束。娜迪亞學到，漂亮句子容易寫，真正說到做到卻不容易。艾利從不要求她掩藏差別。「把『brown』留在詞彙筆記裡吧，」娜迪亞說，「有需要時再確認意思。」在情書筆記本的角落，娜迪亞看見一幅舊畫旁寫著「honey」，於是停下來詢問意思。「stair。」艾利讀出練習頁上的字，接著試著把它和眼前真正看得到的東西連起來。
